## Zed Editor's GPUI Rendering Pipeline & View Architecture

Based on the Zed codebase exploration, here's a comprehensive breakdown of their patterns for your code editor:

---

### **1) View & Widget Structure Patterns**

**Two-Tier System:**

Zed uses a hybrid **immediate + retained mode** architecture with three registers:

```rust
// High-level declarative UI with Views
pub trait Render: 'static + Sized {
    fn render(&mut self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>) -> impl IntoElement;
}

// Mid-level reusable components
pub trait RenderOnce: 'static {
    fn render(self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> impl IntoElement;
}

// Low-level imperative elements
pub trait Element: 'static + IntoElement {
    type RequestLayoutState: 'static;
    type PrepaintState: 'static;

    fn request_layout(...) -> (LayoutId, Self::RequestLayoutState);
    fn prepaint(...) -> Self::PrepaintState;
    fn paint(...);
}
```

**Key Pattern - Entity-based Views:**

Views are actually [`Entity<V>`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/view.rs#L28-L79) handles—reference-counted pointers to state owned by the App. This enables:
- Shared ownership without garbage collection
- Fine-grained reactive updates via `notify()`
- Decoupled state management

```rust
// Entities are created and owned by the App
let my_view = cx.new(|_| MyViewState { /* ... */ });

// Updated through the handle with access to context
my_view.update(cx, |state, cx| {
    state.field = new_value;
    cx.notify(); // Trigger re-render
});
```

**Component Library Pattern:**

The [`ui` crate](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/tree/main/crates/ui/src/components) implements reusable components as `RenderOnce` types using the [`#[derive(IntoElement)]`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/ui/src/components/modal.rs#L9-L17) macro. Example - [Modal component](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/ui/src/components/modal.rs#L9-L92):

```rust
#[derive(IntoElement)]
pub struct Modal {
    id: ElementId,
    header: ModalHeader,
    children: SmallVec<[AnyElement; 2]>,
    // ...
}

impl RenderOnce for Modal {
    fn render(self, _window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) -> impl IntoElement {
        v_flex()
            .id(self.id)
            .size_full()
            .child(self.header)
            .child(/* content */)
            .children(self.footer)
    }
}
```

---

### **2) Layout Computation Pipeline**

**Three-Phase Render Cycle:**

Every frame goes through request_layout → prepaint → paint:

```rust
// Phase 1: REQUEST_LAYOUT
// Taffy computes all layout Ids recursively
fn request_layout(
    &mut self,
    id: Option<&GlobalElementId>,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> (LayoutId, Self::RequestLayoutState);

// Phase 2: PREPAINT
// Hitboxes, tooltips, dispatch tree committed (bounds known)
fn prepaint(
    &mut self,
    id: Option<&GlobalElementId>,
    bounds: Bounds<Pixels>,
    request_layout: &mut Self::RequestLayoutState,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) -> Self::PrepaintState;

// Phase 3: PAINT
// Actually render primitives to scene
fn paint(
    &mut self,
    bounds: Bounds<Pixels>,
    request_layout: &mut Self::RequestLayoutState,
    prepaint: &mut Self::PrepaintState,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
);
```

**Taffy Integration:**

[`TaffyLayoutEngine`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/taffy.rs#L30-L35) wraps the Taffy flexbox layout library. Elements request layouts which are computed depth-first:

```rust
pub fn request_layout(
    &mut self,
    style: Style,
    rem_size: Pixels,
    scale_factor: f32,
    children: &[LayoutId],
) -> LayoutId {
    let taffy_style = style.to_taffy(rem_size, scale_factor);

    if children.is_empty() {
        self.taffy.new_leaf(taffy_style).into()
    } else {
        self.taffy.new_with_children(taffy_style, children).into()
    }
}
```

**Deferred Drawing:**

For overlay/modal patterns, Zed uses `DeferredDraw` to queue elements for rendering after normal element tree:

```rust
pub(crate) struct DeferredDraw {
    current_view: EntityId,
    priority: usize,
    parent_node: DispatchNodeId,
    element_id_stack: SmallVec<[ElementId; 32]>,
    text_style_stack: Vec<TextStyleRefinement>,
    element: Option<AnyElement>,
    absolute_offset: Point<Pixels>,
    prepaint_range: Range<PrepaintStateIndex>,
    paint_range: Range<PaintIndex>,
}
```

---

### **3) Focus & Input Routing for Modals/Overlays**

**Dispatch Tree Architecture:**

[`DispatchTree`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/key_dispatch.rs#L71-L81) maintains a tree of dispatch nodes mapping focus IDs and key contexts. Events propagate from focused element up through hierarchy:

```rust
pub(crate) struct DispatchTree {
    node_stack: Vec<DispatchNodeId>,
    context_stack: Vec<KeyContext>,
    view_stack: Vec<EntityId>,
    nodes: Vec<DispatchNode>,
    focusable_node_ids: FxHashMap<FocusId, DispatchNodeId>,
    view_node_ids: FxHashMap<EntityId, DispatchNodeId>,
    keymap: Rc<RefCell<Keymap>>,
    action_registry: Rc<ActionRegistry>,
}

pub(crate) struct DispatchNode {
    pub key_listeners: Vec<KeyListener>,
    pub action_listeners: Vec<DispatchActionListener>,
    pub context: Option<KeyContext>,
    pub focus_id: Option<FocusId>,
    view_id: Option<EntityId>,
    parent: Option<DispatchNodeId>,
}
```

**Focus Handle System:**

Every focusable element gets a [`FocusHandle`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L272-L350), which is an opaque token that can:
- Track focus state via `FocusId`
- Dispatch actions to the element
- Check containment relationships in the element tree

```rust
pub struct FocusHandle {
    pub(crate) id: FocusId,
    handles: Arc<FocusMap>,
    pub tab_index: isize,
    pub tab_stop: bool,
}

impl FocusHandle {
    pub fn focus(&self, window: &mut Window) { window.focus(self) }
    pub fn is_focused(&self, window: &Window) -> bool { self.id.is_focused(window) }
    pub fn contains(&self, other: &Self, window: &Window) -> bool {
        // Uses dispatch tree to check element containment
        self.id.contains(other.id, window)
    }
    pub fn dispatch_action(&self, action: &dyn Action, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut App) {
        if let Some(node_id) = window.rendered_frame.dispatch_tree.focusable_node_id(self.id) {
            window.dispatch_action_on_node(node_id, action, cx)
        }
    }
}
```

**Modal Pattern with Focus Trapping:**

The [`Focusable` trait](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L447-L456) and [`ManagedView`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L458-L462) enable modal focus management:

```rust
pub trait Focusable: 'static {
    fn focus_handle(&self, cx: &App) -> FocusHandle;
}

pub trait ManagedView: Focusable + EventEmitter<DismissEvent> + Render {}
```

Modals trap focus by:
1. Getting their own `FocusHandle` during render
2. Tracking when focused via `focus_handle.is_focused(window)`
3. Using `.track_focus(&focus_handle)` on the div to mark dispatch tree nodes

```rust
div()
    .track_focus(&modal_focus_handle)
    .on_mouse_down_out(|_, window, cx| {
        // Close modal when clicking outside
        cx.emit(DismissEvent);
    })
    .on_key_down(KeyCode::Escape, |_, window, cx| {
        cx.emit(DismissEvent);
    })
```

**Event Dispatch Phases:**

Events propagate in two phases:

```rust
pub enum DispatchPhase {
    /// Capture: top-down from root toward focused element (special case handling)
    Capture,
    /// Bubble: bottom-up from focused element to root (normal handling)
    Bubble,
}
```

Modals can intercept in capture phase:

```rust
div()
    .on_mouse_down_out(|event, phase, hitbox, window, cx| {
        if phase == DispatchPhase::Capture && !hitbox.contains(&window.mouse_position()) {
            cx.stop_propagation(); // Prevent interaction with elements behind modal
        }
    })
```

**HitBox System for Overlays:**

[`Hitbox`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L532-L573) and [`HitboxBehavior`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L575-L632) control mouse event routing:

```rust
pub enum HitboxBehavior {
    /// Normal - doesn't affect others
    Normal,
    /// BlockMouse - all hitboxes behind are ignored
    BlockMouse,
    /// BlockMouseExceptScroll - block clicks but allow scroll
    BlockMouseExceptScroll,
}

// Used via div().occlude() for modals
div().occlude() // Sets BlockMouse behavior
```

Hit testing is done in reverse order (newest/topmost first):

```rust
pub(crate) fn hit_test(&self, position: Point<Pixels>) -> HitTest {
    let mut hit_test = HitTest::default();
    for hitbox in self.hitboxes.iter().rev() {  // Reverse order!
        let bounds = hitbox.bounds.intersect(&hitbox.content_mask.bounds);
        if bounds.contains(&position) {
            hit_test.ids.push(hitbox.id);
            if hitbox.behavior == HitboxBehavior::BlockMouse {
                break;  // Stop checking behind this hitbox
            }
        }
    }
    hit_test
}
```

---

### **4) GPUI Framework Architecture Overview**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      Application                            │
│  - Owns all Entities via EntityMap                          │
│  - Manages Windows                                          │
│  - Coordinates rendering, input, events                     │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
               │
     ┌─────────┴────────────────┐
     │                          │
  ┌──▼─────────────┐     ┌──────▼──────────────┐
  │  Entity<View>  │     │  Window             │
  │  (Render)      │     │  ┌────────────────┐ │
  │                │     │  │ Frame          │ │
  │  State: T      │     │  │ ┌────────────┐ │ │
  │                │     │  │ │ DispatchTree│ │ │
  │  notify()      │     │  │ │ (focus flow) │ │ │
  │  update(cx)    │     │  │ │────────────┘ │ │
  └────────────────┘     │  │ ┌────────────┐ │ │
                         │  │ │ Scene      │ │ │
                         │  │ │ (primitives)│ │ │
                         │  │ │────────────┘ │ │
                         │  │ ┌────────────┐ │ │
                         │  │ │ Hitboxes   │ │ │
                         │  │ │ (mouse)    │ │ │
                         │  │ └────────────┘ │ │
                         │  └────────────────┘ │
                         │                     │
                         │  render_frame      │
                         │  next_frame        │
                         │  layout_engine     │
                         └─────────────────────┘
                               │
              ┌────────────────┬┴─────────────┐
              │                │              │
         ┌────▼────────┐  ┌────▼───────┐  ┌──▼──────┐
         │   Render    │  │   Layout   │  │  Paint  │
         │   (Views)   │  │  (Taffy)   │  │ (Scene) │
         │             │  │            │  │         │
         │ request_    │  │ request_   │  │ paint() │
         │ layout()    │  │ layout()   │  │         │
         │             │  │ compute()  │  │ insert_ │
         │ impl Element│  │            │  │ primitive│
         └─────────────┘  └────────────┘  └─────────┘
                               │
         ┌─────────────────────┴──────────────────┐
         │                                        │
    ┌────▼──────────┐    ┌─────────────────────┐ │
    │  ElementIdStack│    │   Element Tree      │ │
    │                │    │  (parent/children)  │ │
    └────────────────┘    └─────────────────────┘ │
                                                   │
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │
    ┌────▼──────────────────────────────┐
    │  TextStyleStack                    │
    │  ContentMaskStack                  │
    │  ElementOpacityStack               │
    │  (Applied during element layout)   │
    └────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Render Cycle Per Frame:**

```rust
// 1. EVENT DISPATCH (before render)
window.dispatch_action(action, cx)  // Routes to focused element's dispatch node

// 2. VIEW NOTIFICATION
view.update(cx, |state, cx| {
    // modify state
    cx.notify();  // Mark view as dirty
})

// 3. RENDER PHASE
// Root view's render() is called
impl Render for MyView {
    fn render(&mut self, window: &mut Window, cx: &mut Context<Self>) -> impl IntoElement {
        div()
            .track_focus(&self.focus_handle)        // Register in dispatch tree
            .on_action(cx.listener(Self::handle))   // Register action listener
            .child(/* element tree */)
    }
}

// 4. LAYOUT PHASE
// For each element depth-first:
element.request_layout(window, cx)  // Returns LayoutId from Taffy

// 5. PREPAINT PHASE
// For each element in layout order:
element.prepaint(bounds, window, cx)  // Hitbox commit, state cache

// 6. PAINT PHASE
// For each element in reverse order (back to front):
element.paint(bounds, window, cx)  // Add primitives to Scene

// 7. GPU RENDERING
window.present()  // Send Scene to GPU via platform backend
```

**Key Dependencies:**

- **[`Entity<V>`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/app/entity_map.rs)** - Reference-counted handle to App-owned state
- **[`Element`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/element.rs#L47-L110)** - Trait for layout/paint control
- **[`Render`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/element.rs#L131-L134)** - Trait for views to construct element trees
- **[`Style`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/style.rs)** - Tailwind-like styling API
- **[`Window`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L832-L891)** - Per-window state, layout engine, rendering context
- **[`Frame`](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/gpui/src/window.rs#L674-L695)** - Single frame's render data (dispatch tree, scene, hitboxes)

---

### **Applicable Patterns for Your Elm-Architecture Rust Editor**

**Recommended Adaptations:**

1. **Replace pure Elm with Entity-based state:**
   - Instead of immutable state snapshots, use `Entity<ModelState>`
   - Call `model.update(cx, |state, cx| { /* model function */ })`
   - Leverage fine-grained reactivity via `notify()`

2. **Adopt the three-tier element system:**
   - **Views** (Elm-like `Render` trait) for stateful components
   - **Components** (RenderOnce) for pure data-driven UI
   - **Custom Elements** only for editor rendering (syntax highlighting, selection, etc.)

3. **Use DispatchTree for keybinding/action routing:**
   - Define actions as structs implementing `Action`
   - Register with `.on_action(cx.listener(handler))`
   - Let focus hierarchy determine which handlers fire

4. **Implement modal/overlay via hitbox blocking:**
   - Use `.occlude()` to block events to background elements
   - Use `DeferredDraw` for rendering above normal tree
   - Track modal focus with `FocusHandle` for ESC/outside-click dismissal

---

This architecture gives you GPU-accelerated, low-latency rendering with Elm-like state management transparency while maintaining Rust's safety guarantees through ownership.

## Rendering Architecture Comparison: Alacritty vs Wezterm

Based on my analysis of both codebases, here's a detailed breakdown of how these terminal emulators handle rendering:

### 1) Display/Grid Rendering Structure

#### **Alacritty** ([display/mod.rs](https://github.com/alacritty/alacritty/blob/master/alacritty/src/display/mod.rs), [renderer/mod.rs](https://github.com/alacritty/alacritty/blob/master/alacritty/src/renderer/mod.rs))

**Architecture:**
- **Display system** owns the window, glyph cache, and damage tracker
- **Renderer abstraction** supports both GLES2 and GLSL3, chosen at runtime based on GPU capability
- **SizeInfo struct** encapsulates viewport dimensions, cell metrics, and padding
- **RenderableContent iterator** walks grid cells, filtering empty cells and computing colors/flags on-the-fly

**Cell rendering pipeline:**
```
Terminal Grid → RenderableContent (iterator)
    ↓
Filter empty cells + compute cell state (color, flags, decorations)
    ↓
draw_cells() for text
    ↓
TextRenderBatch (gathers glyphs by texture atlas)
    ↓
draw_rects() for underlines/strikeout/cursors
    ↓
OpenGL rendering via GLES2/GLSL3 renderer
```

**Key characteristics:**
- Each cell becomes a `RenderableCell` with computed colors
- Uses **dual-pass rendering**: text glyphs first, then decorative rectangles
- Glyph batching by texture atlas to minimize shader switches
- Text vertex/index buffers hold computed geometry (position + texture coords)

#### **Wezterm** ([termwindow/mod.rs](https://github.com/wezterm/wezterm/blob/main/wezterm-gui/src/termwindow/mod.rs), [renderstate.rs](https://github.com/wezterm/wezterm/blob/main/wezterm-gui/src/renderstate.rs))

**Architecture:**
- **TermWindow** is monolithic, owning render state, panes, tabs, and UI overlays
- **RenderContext abstraction** supports both Glium (OpenGL) and WebGPU backends
- **Quad-based rendering**: every visual element (text, decoration, background) becomes a vertex quad
- **Triple-buffered vertex buffers** for GPU streaming with dynamic reallocation
- **Layered rendering system**: depth-ordered z-index layers for UI composition

**Cell rendering pipeline:**
```
Terminal Grid → per-pane rendering
    ↓
paint_pane() → render_screen_line()
    ↓
For each cell: allocate quad from layer's allocator
    ↓
Fill quad vertices with position, texture coords, colors, HSV
    ↓
Multiple layers rendered in z-order
    ↓
GPU submission (Glium or WebGPU)
```

**Key characteristics:**
- **Quad-first design**: 4 vertices per cell (constant geometry)
- **All-in-one quads**: foreground, background, decorations, and effects in single vertex
- Layer-based z-ordering allows true overlay support
- Supports WebGPU for future-proofing

---

### 2) Damage Tracking & Partial Redraw Patterns

#### **Alacritty** ([display/damage.rs](https://github.com/alacritty/alacritty/blob/master/alacritty/src/display/damage.rs))

**Design: Line-based granular damage**

```rust
pub struct DamageTracker {
    frames: [FrameDamage; 2],  // Double-buffered
}

pub struct FrameDamage {
    full: bool,                        // Full redraw needed
    lines: Vec<LineDamageBounds>,      // Per-line damage (left..right column range)
    rects: Vec<Rect>,                  // Extra UI rects (message bar, search)
}
```

**Damage collection process:**

1. **Terminal damage**: Uses `TermDamage` from terminal crate
   - Full or Partial(damaged_lines) from terminal state changes
   - Tracks line-level damage ranges (columns affected)

2. **UI element damage**:
   - Vi mode cursor position changes
   - Selection range changes
   - Visual bell intensity
   - Search bar/message bar rendering

3. **Rectangle merging optimization**:
   ```rust
   // Merges overlapping damage rects to reduce GPU submissions
   let mut total_damage_rect = overdamage(size_info, rect);
   while overlapping_rects {
       total_damage_rect = merge_rects(total_damage_rect, next_rect);
   }
   ```

4. **Wide character handling**:
   - Overdamages adjacent cells to account for multi-cell glyphs
   - Expands damage by ±cell width horizontally, ±cell height/2 vertically

**Wayland optimization**:
- Calls `swap_buffers_with_damage()` on Wayland with computed damage rects
- X11/macOS always does full buffer swap (less efficient)

**Frame lifecycle**:
```
Frame N rendering with damage from Frame N-1
    ↓
damage_tracker.frame() += new damage
    ↓
shape_frame_damage() converts line damages → screen rects
    ↓
OpenGL rendering only affected regions
    ↓
damage_tracker.swap_damage() → advance to next frame
```

#### **Wezterm** (quad-based, no explicit damage tracking)

**Design: Quad allocation as implicit damage**

Wezterm uses a different philosophy:
- **No explicit damage tracking** in classical sense
- **Quad reallocation on each frame** serves as implicit damage tracking
- Only quads that are allocated get rendered

**Optimization strategy:**

1. **Quad allocator pattern**:
   ```rust
   pub struct MappedQuads<'a> {
       mapping: MappedVertexBuffer,
       next: RefMut<'a, usize>,  // Tracks next quad index
       capacity: usize,
   }
   ```

2. **Texture atlas management**:
   - Detects `OutOfTextureSpace` and dynamically grows atlas
   - Multi-pass rendering if texture grows mid-frame
   - Gracefully degrades image quality (scale, disable) if space exhausted

3. **Layer-based partial rendering**:
   ```rust
   for layer in layers {
       layer.clear_quad_allocation();  // Clear from previous frame
       // Redraw only what goes into this layer
   }
   ```

4. **Viewport optimization**:
   - Only renders visible panes (splits, tabs)
   - Caches shaped text lines to avoid reshaping on scroll

**Strengths**:
- Simpler architecture (no damage rect book-keeping)
- GPU naturally batches only allocated quads
- Better for complex UIs with many overlays

**Tradeoffs**:
- Allocates quads even for unchanged content
- No benefit from true partial framebuffer updates
- Higher GPU submission overhead

---

### 3) Overlay/Popup Handling

#### **Alacritty**

**Simple rect-based approach:**

1. **Message bar** (errors, warnings):
   - Rendered as solid color `RenderRect`
   - Added to damage on each frame (unpredictable size/duration)

2. **Search bar**:
   - Drawn as string text via `draw_string()`
   - Reduces viewport lines to reserve space
   - Damages all lines each frame while active

3. **Hint highlighting**:
   - URLs highlighted with underlines
   - Damaged on mouse/vi-cursor position changes
   - Full redraw if hint becomes invalid (grid changed)

4. **Visual bell**:
   - Overlay rect covering entire window
   - Full damage while intensity > 0

**Overlay architecture**: Overlays are implicitly handled—they trigger damage that causes full or partial redraws, not true compositional layers.

#### **Wezterm**

**Advanced layer-based composition:**

1. **Z-index layering system**:
   ```rust
   pub fn layer_for_zindex(&self, zindex: usize) -> Layer {
       // Returns layer ordered by depth
   }
   ```

2. **UI overlays as first-class citizens**:
   - Launcher popup
   - Copy mode overlay
   - Character selector
   - Pane selector
   - Confirmation dialogs
   - Palette/search

3. **Each overlay can**:
   - Render to dedicated z-index layer
   - Capture mouse/keyboard input
   - Have independent lifecycle
   - Be animated with smooth transitions

4. **Composition example** (from [mod.rs](https://github.com/wezterm/wezterm/blob/main/wezterm-gui/src/termwindow/mod.rs)):
   ```rust
   // Render background/terminal panes
   for pane in panes {
       paint_pane(pane, layers)?;
   }

   // Render UI overlays at higher z-indices
   for overlay in overlays {
       paint_overlay(overlay, layers)?;
   }

   // GPU renders all layers in z-order
   ```

**Advantages**:
- Clean separation of concerns
- Complex UIs without damage tracking complexity
- Overlays don't need to invalidate terminal content
- Supports true modal dialogs

---

### 4) GPU Rendering Approach

#### **Alacritty** (OpenGL-centric)

**Shader selection**:
- **GLSL 3.3+**: Uses `Glsl3Renderer` with modern features
- **GLES 2.0 fallback**: For older GPUs, uses `Gles2Renderer`
- Detects at runtime, no user configuration needed

**Text rendering** ([text/mod.rs](https://github.com/alacritty/alacritty/blob/master/alacritty/src/renderer/text/mod.rs)):
```rust
pub trait TextRenderBatch {
    fn add_item(&mut self, cell: &RenderableCell, glyph: &Glyph, size_info: &SizeInfo);
}

// Glyph batching by texture
for cell in cells {
    if batch.tex() != glyph.tex_id {
        render_batch();  // Flush on texture change
    }
    batch.add_item(&cell, &glyph, size_info);
}
```

**Decorations** ([rects.rs](https://github.com/alacritty/alacritty/blob/master/alacritty/src/renderer/rects.rs)):
- Separate shader program for rectangles (underline, strikeout, cursor)
- Uses different blend modes for rendering
- Combined with text via: text pass → clear blend state → rect pass

**Projection matrix**:
```rust
fn update_projection(u_projection: GLint, size: &SizeInfo) {
    // Converts pixel space [0, width] to NDC [-1, 1]
    // Y-axis inverted (OpenGL convention)
    let scale_x = 2. / (width - 2. * padding_x);
    let scale_y = -2. / (height - 2. * padding_y);
}
```

**Buffer management**:
- Text VBO: `STREAM_DRAW` (CPU → GPU each frame)
- Indices: `STATIC_DRAW` (pre-computed quads)
- Glyph atlases: texture arrays, dynamically grown

#### **Wezterm** (Dual backend: Glium + WebGPU)

**Backend abstraction** ([renderstate.rs](https://github.com/wezterm/wezterm/blob/main/wezterm-gui/src/renderstate.rs)):

```rust
pub enum RenderContext {
    Glium(Rc<GliumContext>),
    WebGpu(Rc<WebGpuState>),
}

// Implementations for both:
pub fn allocate_vertex_buffer(&self, num_quads: usize) -> VertexBuffer {
    match self {
        Self::Glium(ctx) => VertexBuffer::Glium(...),
        Self::WebGpu(state) => VertexBuffer::WebGpu(...),
    }
}
```

**Unified vertex format** ([quad.rs](https://github.com/wezterm/wezterm/blob/main/wezterm-gui/src/quad.rs)):
```rust
#[repr(C)]
pub struct Vertex {
    pub position: [f32; 2],           // Cell position
    pub tex: [f32; 2],                // Texture coordinate
    pub fg_color: [f32; 4],           // Foreground RGBA
    pub alt_color: [f32; 4],          // Underline/alt color
    pub hsv: [f32; 3],                // For inactive pane desaturation
    pub has_color: f32,               // Glyph type flag
    pub mix_value: f32,               // Blending factor
}
```

**Quad type flags**:
- `0.0` = monochrome glyph
- `1.0` = color emoji
- `2.0` = background image
- `3.0` = solid color rect
- `4.0` = grayscale polyquad

**Texture atlas**:
- Dynamically grows; on exhaustion, recreates with larger size
- If GPU texture limit hit, scales down images and retries
- Graceful degradation: `AllowImage::Yes → Scale(2) → Scale(4) → No`

**Shader capabilities**:
- Single shader handles all quad types (branching on has_color)
- Supports HSV adjustments in-shader for inactive panes
- Dual-source blending for subpixel antialiasing

---

### 5) Key Architectural Differences

| Aspect | Alacritty | Wezterm |
|--------|-----------|---------|
| **Primary optimization** | Line-level damage tracking | Quad allocation efficiency |
| **Overlay support** | Implicit (damage-driven) | Explicit z-index layers |
| **Shader approach** | Dual-pass (text + rects) | Single unified quad shader |
| **Buffer strategy** | STREAM_DRAW (CPU-intensive) | Triple-buffered mapping |
| **GPU backend** | OpenGL only | Glium + WebGPU |
| **Partial redraw** | Wayland-only (swap_buffers_with_damage) | Implicit via quad allocation |
| **Texture management** | Pre-computed atlas | Dynamic grow on demand |
| **Wide char handling** | Over-damage adjacent cells | Part of vertex data |
| **Color effects** | Limited (vim cursor, hints) | Rich (HSV, mixing, effects) |

---

### 6) Lessons for Your Softbuffer Editor

Given you're building with CPU rendering (softbuffer):

1. **Adopt Alacritty's damage model**:
   - Track line-level or region-based dirty bits
   - Only redraw changed regions
   - Merge adjacent damage rectangles to reduce fragmentation

2. **Consider overlays early**:
   - Define z-index layers for popups/menus
   - Don't let overlays force full redraws
   - Use explicit composition rather than implicit damage

3. **Batch geometry**:
   - Group cells by rendering properties (background color, text style)
   - Sort by texture/color to minimize state changes
   - Pre-compute glyph metrics

4. **Memory-efficient caching**:
   - Cache shaped text lines (width, breaks, clusters)
   - Use arena allocators for frame-local objects
   - Pre-allocate grid for known viewport size

5. **Watch out for CPU-GPU synchronization**:
   - Softbuffer reads from VRAM, which stalls if GPU is still rendering
   - Consider multiple backbuffers like Wezterm
   - Profile CPU→GPU transfer overhead

Would you like me to dive deeper into any specific area (damage rect merging algorithm, glyph cache architecture, or overlay composition patterns)?

## File Organization for Reference

Here's the structure you explored:

- **[helix-term/src/compositor.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/compositor.rs)** - Core Component trait and Compositor
- **[helix-term/src/ui/overlay.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/overlay.rs)** - Overlay wrapper for positioning
- **[helix-term/src/ui/popup.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/popup.rs)** - Sophisticated popup positioning logic
- **[helix-term/src/ui/prompt.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/prompt.rs)** - Input prompt with completion
- **[helix-term/src/ui/picker.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/picker.rs)** - Fuzzy picker modal
- **[helix-term/src/ui/menu.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/menu.rs)** - Completion menu (simpler menu widget)
- **[helix-view/src/view.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-view/src/view.rs)** - View with coordinate transformation logic
- **[helix-tui/src/buffer.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-tui/src/buffer.rs)** - Buffer/Surface abstraction (Cell-based)
- **[helix-tui/src/terminal.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-tui/src/terminal.rs)** - Double-buffering and diff optimization
- **[helix-term/src/ui/editor.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/ui/editor.rs)** - EditorView component with rendering orchestration
- **[helix-term/src/application.rs](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/main/helix-term/src/application.rs)** - Main event loop and render orchestration

---

## Key Insights for Your Winit/Softbuffer Implementation

1. **Decouple Components from Rendering Backend**: Helix's components don't know about terminal cells or pixels - they work with abstract `Rect` and `Surface`. This makes them reusable.

2. **EventResult + Callback Pattern**: This allows the compositor to be modified as a side effect of event handling, enabling dynamic UI changes (push modal, pop prompt, etc.) without returning from the event handler.

3. **Anchor-Based Scrolling**: Much more efficient than tracking line ranges, especially with soft-wrapping and virtual text.

4. **Cursor Priority Over Drawing**: The topmost component that provides a cursor wins. This lets you have keyboard focus follow the UI naturally.

5. **Double-Buffering with Diff**: Helix only sends changed cells to the terminal. For softbuffer, you could do the same by comparing pixel buffers or use a dirty-rect system.

6. **Required Size Negotiation**: Allows complex layouts where children report their constraints and parents allocate space. This is how popups and overlays size themselves.

1) **TL;DR**

Incrementally evolve the GUI by:
(1) introducing a small `Frame`/`Painter` abstraction and extracting widgets,
(2) adding a simple layer/compositor pass,
(3) wiring a modal/focus system, starting with a minimal command palette,
(4) then layering in goto line / find‑replace and finally damage tracking once the architecture has settled.

---

2) **Recommended approach (simple phased plan)**

Effort legend: **S** <1h, **M** 1–3h, **L** 1–2d, **XL** >2d

I’ll assume the codebase state described in `ORGANIZATION-CODEBASE.md` is current.

---

### Phase 1 – Frame/Painter abstraction (no behavior change)

**Goal:** Stop indexing the pixel buffer everywhere; centralize drawing primitives in a `Frame` + text painter.
**Effort:** **M** (1–3h)
**User impact:** None (internal refactor, but unblocks everything else).

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From GUI-REVIEW: “single painter context” (egui/Druid-style).
- **Skip**: Zed’s full 3‑phase `layout/prepaint/paint` for now; you can simulate this later if needed.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Introduce `Frame` and `TextPainter` in `view.rs`.**

   - Add at top of `view.rs`:

     ```rust
     pub struct Frame<'a> {
         pub buffer: &'a mut [u32],
         pub width: usize,
         pub height: usize,
     }

     impl<'a> Frame<'a> {
         pub fn clear(&mut self, color: u32) { /* from GUI-REVIEW */ }
         pub fn fill_rect(&mut self, rect: Rect, color: u32) { /* from GUI-REVIEW */ }
         pub fn blend_pixel(&mut self, x: usize, y: usize, color: u32) { /* wrapper */ }
         // more as needed
     }

     pub struct TextPainter<'a> {
         font: &'a Font,          // or your fontdue-specific type
         glyph_cache: &'a mut GlyphCache,
         // theme or colors as needed
     }

     impl<'a> TextPainter<'a> {
         pub fn draw_text(&mut self, frame: &mut Frame, pos: Point, text: &str, color: u32) {
             // move existing draw_text logic here
         }
     }
     ```

2. **Wrap the existing render entrypoint with `Frame`.**

   - In `Renderer::render_impl` (or equivalent), currently you get `&mut [u32]` from softbuffer:
     ```rust
     let mut frame = Frame { buffer, width, height };
     let mut text_painter = TextPainter::new(&self.font, &mut self.glyph_cache);
     render_root(&mut frame, &mut text_painter, model, &self.theme);
     ```
   - `render_root` is your existing “draw everything” function (can still live in `view.rs`).

3. **Migrate existing pixel loops to `Frame` methods.**

   - Replace manual loops with `frame.fill_rect` and `text_painter.draw_text` in:
     - tab bar drawing
     - status bar
     - gutter + text area backgrounds
     - splitters
     - perf overlay (`perf.rs` – add a small adapter `Frame` reference).

4. **Refactor `overlay.rs` to use `Frame` helpers.**

   - Change overlay utilities to take `&mut Frame` instead of `&mut [u32]`:
     ```rust
     pub fn render_overlay_background(frame: &mut Frame, rect: Rect, color: u32) { ... }
     pub fn render_overlay_border(frame: &mut Frame, rect: Rect, color: u32) { ... }
     ```
   - Internally, just call `fill_rect` / `blend_pixel`.

5. **Compile + run tests after each group of conversions.**

   - Do this in small chunks (status bar first, then tab bar, etc.) so regressions are obvious.

---

### Phase 2 – Widget extraction & geometry centralization

**Goal:** Turn the monolithic `render_all` into a small set of composable widget functions with consistent geometry APIs.
**Effort:** **M–L** (3–8h, but can be chipped away)
**User impact:** Still mostly invisible, but greatly improves maintainability.

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From Helix: Component‑per‑concern with a simple compositor (no traits necessary yet).
- From GUI-REVIEW & EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE: centralize geometry math (tabs, gutter, text area).
- **Skip**: Helix’s full trait-based `Component` / callback system for now.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Define a `view` module structure if `view.rs` grows again.**

   - Optional right now, but you can prepare:
     - `view/mod.rs` – exports `Renderer`, `Frame`, `TextPainter`, render entrypoints.
     - `view/editor.rs` – editor area widgets.
     - `view/chrome.rs` – tab bar, status bar.
     - `view/modals.rs` – later, for overlays.
   - For now, you can stay with a single `view.rs` until ~1200–1400 LOC, then split.

2. **Extract high-level widget renderers.**

   In `view.rs`, define:

   ```rust
   pub fn render_root(
       frame: &mut Frame,
       text: &mut TextPainter,
       model: &AppModel,
       theme: &Theme,
   ) {
       render_editor_area(frame, text, &model.editor_area, theme);
       render_status_bar(frame, text, &model.status_bar, theme);
       // modals & perf overlay later
   }

   fn render_editor_area(...) { /* only editor groups & splitters */ }
   fn render_editor_group(...) { /* one group: tab bar + editor pane */ }
   fn render_tab_bar(...) { /* no document logic, pure view */ }
   fn render_gutter(...) { /* line numbers, breakpoints, etc. */ }
   fn render_text_area(...) { /* text, cursors, selection */ }
   fn render_splitters(...) { ... }
   fn render_status_bar(...) { ... }
   ```

3. **Centralize geometry helpers.**

   - Use `EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md` as the source of truth:
     - Extract helpers for:
       - computing visible lines for a viewport (`compute_visible_lines`),
       - line/column ↔ pixel conversions,
       - gutter width computation.
   - Put them either in `model/editor.rs` (if truly model-ish) or a new `view/geometry.rs`.

4. **Unify hit-testing geometry between `input.rs` and `view.rs`.**

   - Introduce functions in a shared module (e.g. `model/editor_area.rs` or `view/geometry.rs`) that compute:
     - tab bar rect per group
     - text area rect per group
     - gutter rect per group
   - Use these identically from:
     - `render_*` functions
     - mouse handling in `input.rs`.

5. **Compile + run tests.**

   - After each widget extraction (tab bar, then status bar, then editor group), make sure everything still works.

---

### Phase 3 – Basic modal/focus system (no command palette yet)

**Goal:** Add a minimal, generic modal overlay + focus capture mechanism that doesn’t change much else.
**Effort:** **M** (1–3h)
**User impact:** Foundations only; you might add a dummy “About” modal to prove the path.

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From Zed: **modal focus capture** (when a modal is active, it owns keyboard input).
- From Helix: **overlay wrapper** + layer stack (we’ll implement a simplified version).
- **Skip**: Zed’s `DispatchTree` / entity system; overkill for your Elm core.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Extend the model (`model/ui.rs`).**

   - Add:

     ```rust
     pub enum ModalState {
         CommandPalette(CommandPaletteState), // later
         GotoLine(GotoLineState),
         FindReplace(FindReplaceState),
         // For now, add a simple Placeholder modal:
         Placeholder(String),
     }

     pub struct UiState {
         pub active_modal: Option<ModalState>,
         // existing fields: cursor blink, status, etc.
     }
     ```

   - Initially, you can only implement `Placeholder` to test the pipeline.

2. **Extend messages (`messages.rs`).**

   - Extend `UiMsg` and `Msg`:

     ```rust
     pub enum ModalMsg {
         // variant per modal type, even if empty for now
         PlaceholderOk,
         PlaceholderCancel,
         CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg),
         GotoLine(GotoLineMsg),
         FindReplace(FindReplaceMsg),
     }

     pub enum UiMsg {
         // existing ...
         OpenPlaceholderModal(String),
         CloseModal,
         Modal(ModalMsg),
     }
     ```

3. **Handle modal state in `update/ui.rs`.**

   - Implement something close to what’s sketched in `GUI-REVIEW-FINDINGS.md`:

     ```rust
     pub fn update_ui(model: &mut AppModel, msg: UiMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
         match msg {
             UiMsg::OpenPlaceholderModal(text) => {
                 model.ui.active_modal = Some(ModalState::Placeholder(text));
                 Some(Cmd::Redraw)
             }
             UiMsg::CloseModal => {
                 model.ui.active_modal = None;
                 Some(Cmd::Redraw)
             }
             UiMsg::Modal(m) => {
                 update_modal(model, m);
                 Some(Cmd::Redraw)
             }
             // existing cases...
         }
     }

     fn update_modal(model: &mut AppModel, msg: ModalMsg) {
         match (&mut model.ui.active_modal, msg) {
             (Some(ModalState::Placeholder(_)), ModalMsg::PlaceholderCancel) => {
                 model.ui.active_modal = None;
             }
             // later: CommandPalette, GotoLine, FindReplace
             _ => {}
         }
     }
     ```

4. **Implement keyboard focus capture in `input.rs`.**

   - Wrap the current key handling:

     ```rust
     pub fn handle_key(model: &AppModel, event: &KeyEvent) -> Option<Msg> {
         if model.ui.active_modal.is_some() {
             return handle_modal_key(model, event);
         }
         handle_editor_key(model, event)
     }
     ```

   - Initially `handle_modal_key` can just:
     - ESC → `UiMsg::CloseModal`
     - Enter → `UiMsg::Modal(PlaceholderOk)`
     - Ignore everything else.

5. **Render a simple modal rectangle on top in `view.rs`.**

   - Add:

     ```rust
     pub fn render_root(...) {
         render_editor_area(...);
         render_status_bar(...);
         render_modals(frame, text, &model.ui, theme);
         render_perf_overlay(...); // if debug
     }

     fn render_modals(
         frame: &mut Frame,
         text: &mut TextPainter,
         ui: &UiState,
         theme: &Theme,
     ) {
         let Some(modal) = &ui.active_modal else { return };

         // 1. Dim background (Zed-style BlockMouse)
         let dim_color = 0x80000000;
         for pixel in frame.buffer.iter_mut() {
             *pixel = blend_pixel(dim_color, *pixel);
         }

         // 2. Render modal box
         match modal {
             ModalState::Placeholder(msg) => {
                 // center a rect, draw border/text using overlay.rs helpers
             }
             _ => {}
         }
     }
     ```

   - This is your first “layer”: editor content, dimmer, then modal box.

6. **Test focus capture.**

   - Add a temporary keybinding (e.g. `F1`) that triggers `UiMsg::OpenPlaceholderModal("Hello")`.
   - Verify:
     - Arrow keys don’t move the editor while modal is open.
     - ESC closes the modal.

---

### Phase 4 – Command palette infrastructure (full vertical slice)

**Goal:** Ship a real, useful command palette on top of the modal system.
**Effort:** **L** (1–2d; this is where most wiring lives)
**User impact:** High – visible feature, anchors the modal system.

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From GUI-REVIEW: `CommandId` enum + static `COMMANDS` registry.
- From Zed: palette as a picker for commands (simple “PickerDelegate”-like design).
- **Skip**: dynamic plugin discovery or workspace‑introspective commands for now.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Extend `commands.rs`.**

   - Add:

     ```rust
     #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
     pub enum CommandId {
         ShowCommandPalette,
         GotoLine,
         FindInFile,
         SaveFile,
         SplitRight,
         SplitDown,
         Undo,
         Redo,
         // ... map only the commands you actually wire
     }

     pub struct Command {
         pub id: CommandId,
         pub label: &'static str,
         pub description: &'static str,
         pub default_keybinding: Option<&'static str>, // purely for docs/labels
     }

     pub static COMMANDS: &[Command] = &[
         Command {
             id: CommandId::ShowCommandPalette,
             label: "Show Command Palette",
             description: "Open the command palette",
             default_keybinding: Some("Cmd+P"),
         },
         // ...
     ];
     ```

2. **Add `AppMsg::ExecuteCommand` path.**

   - In `messages.rs`:

     ```rust
     pub enum AppMsg {
         // existing ...
         ExecuteCommand(CommandId),
     }
     ```

   - In `update/app.rs`:

     ```rust
     pub fn update_app(model: &mut AppModel, msg: AppMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
         match msg {
             AppMsg::ExecuteCommand(id) => execute_command(model, id),
             // existing ...
         }
     }

     fn execute_command(model: &mut AppModel, cmd_id: CommandId) -> Option<Cmd> {
         use CommandId::*;
         match cmd_id {
             ShowCommandPalette => {
                 model.ui.active_modal = Some(ModalState::CommandPalette(
                     CommandPaletteState::new()
                 ));
                 Some(Cmd::Redraw)
             }
             GotoLine => {
                 model.ui.active_modal = Some(ModalState::GotoLine(
                     GotoLineState::new()
                 ));
                 Some(Cmd::Redraw)
             }
             SaveFile => update_app(model, AppMsg::SaveFile),
             SplitRight => update_layout(model, LayoutMsg::SplitFocusedHorizontal),
             Undo => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Undo),
             // ...
         }
     }
     ```

     (Or reuse the helper version from `GUI-REVIEW-FINDINGS.md`.)

3. **Introduce command palette model types.**

   - In a new `modals/command_palette.rs` or inside `model/ui.rs`:

     ```rust
     pub struct CommandPaletteState {
         pub query: String,
         pub matches: Vec<usize>,     // indices into COMMANDS
         pub selected_index: usize,
     }

     impl CommandPaletteState {
         pub fn new() -> Self { /* initial query empty, matches = all commands */ }
         pub fn recompute_matches(&mut self) { /* simple case-insensitive substring match */ }
     }

     pub enum CommandPaletteMsg {
         InsertChar(char),
         Backspace,
         SelectionUp,
         SelectionDown,
         Confirm,
         Cancel,
     }
     ```

   - Wire into `ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg)`.

4. **Implement update logic for the palette.**

   - As in GUI-REVIEW sample:

     ```rust
     fn update_modal(model: &mut AppModel, msg: ModalMsg) {
         match (&mut model.ui.active_modal, msg) {
             (Some(ModalState::CommandPalette(state)), ModalMsg::CommandPalette(m)) => {
                 update_command_palette(model, state, m);
             }
             // other modals...
             _ => {}
         }
     }

     fn update_command_palette(
         model: &mut AppModel,
         state: &mut CommandPaletteState,
         msg: CommandPaletteMsg,
     ) {
         use CommandPaletteMsg::*;
         match msg {
             InsertChar(ch) => { state.query.push(ch); state.recompute_matches(); }
             Backspace => { state.query.pop(); state.recompute_matches(); }
             SelectionUp => { /* saturating_sub */ }
             SelectionDown => { /* clamp to len-1 */ }
             Confirm => {
                 if let Some(&cmd_idx) = state.matches.get(state.selected_index) {
                     let cmd_id = COMMANDS[cmd_idx].id;
                     model.ui.active_modal = None;
                     execute_command(model, cmd_id);
                 }
             }
             Cancel => { model.ui.active_modal = None; }
         }
     }
     ```

5. **Hook keyboard routing for the palette (`input.rs`).**

   - Extend `handle_modal_key`:

     ```rust
     fn handle_modal_key(model: &AppModel, event: &KeyEvent) -> Option<Msg> {
         use winit::keyboard::{Key, NamedKey};
         if event.state != ElementState::Pressed { return None; }

         match &event.logical_key {
             Key::Named(NamedKey::Escape) => Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::CloseModal)),

             Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowUp) => Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                 ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::SelectionUp)
             ))),

             Key::Named(NamedKey::ArrowDown) => Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                 ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::SelectionDown)
             ))),

             Key::Named(NamedKey::Enter) => Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                 ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::Confirm)
             ))),

             Key::Named(NamedKey::Backspace) => Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                 ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::Backspace)
             ))),

             Key::Character(s) => {
                 if let Some(ch) = s.chars().next() {
                     if !ch.is_control() {
                         return Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                             ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::InsertChar(ch))
                         )));
                     }
                 }
                 None
             }
             _ => None,
         }
     }
     ```

   - Add global keybinding in `input.rs` for `Cmd+P` → `Msg::App(AppMsg::ExecuteCommand(CommandId::ShowCommandPalette))` when no modal active.

6. **Render the command palette (`view/modals.rs`).**

   - Use the pattern in `GUI-REVIEW-FINDINGS.md`:
     - Dim background.
     - Compute a centered rect with fixed height (e.g. 40–60% of width, theme‑driven).
     - Use `overlay.rs` to draw background + border.
     - Render:
       - query line with prompt symbol (`>`),
       - list of top N matches with highlight bar at `selected_index`.

7. **Test the full flow.**

   - Open palette, type to filter, arrow up/down, hit Enter.
   - Confirm that commands execute existing behaviors (save file, split, etc.) via `execute_command`.

---

### Phase 5 – General overlay/compositor & mouse blocking

**Goal:** Make overlays/modals “first‑class layers” with predictable z‑order and event behavior.
**Effort:** **M** (1–3h)
**User impact:** Subtle – more polished behavior (e.g., clicks don’t leak through modals).

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From Helix: layer stack with forward render / reverse event dispatch.
- From Zed: hitbox + `BlockMouse` behavior for overlays.
- **Skip**: Wezterm’s GPU‑oriented quad/layer machinery.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Introduce a tiny compositor concept in `view.rs`.**

   - This can be purely conceptual – a function ordering:

     ```rust
     pub fn render_root(...) {
         render_editor_area(...);     // base layer 0
         render_status_bar(...);      // layer 1
         render_modals(...);          // layer 2 (if any)
         render_perf_overlay(...);    // layer 3 (debug)
     }
     ```

   - If you want a struct:

     ```rust
     struct Layer {
         id: LayerId,
         z: i32,
         // maybe a closure or enum with data
     }
     // but you probably don’t need this yet – simple ordering is enough.
     ```

2. **Add basic hitbox metadata for modals.**

   - In `UiState` or `ModalState`, track the screen rect of the active modal for hit-testing, e.g.:

     ```rust
     pub struct ModalRuntimeGeometry {
         pub rect: Rect,
     }

     pub struct UiState {
         pub active_modal: Option<ModalState>,
         pub active_modal_geometry: Option<ModalRuntimeGeometry>,
         // ...
     }
     ```

   - Update `render_modals` to recompute and store this rect (or compute it in a shared helper used by both render and input).

3. **Implement mouse blocking in `input.rs`.**

   - In your mouse event handler:

     ```rust
     pub fn handle_mouse(model: &AppModel, event: &MouseEvent) -> Option<Msg> {
         if let Some(geom) = &model.ui.active_modal_geometry {
             if point_in_rect(event.position, geom.rect) {
                 // In the future: route to modal (e.g., clicking in search results)
                 return Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(
                     ModalMsg::CommandPalette(CommandPaletteMsg::Click(/*...*/))
                 )));
             } else {
                 // Click outside modal: either close it or ignore click completely
                 return Some(Msg::Ui(UiMsg::CloseModal));
             }
         }

         // no modal active → normal routing
         handle_editor_mouse(model, event)
     }
     ```

4. **Confirm overlay ordering and behavior.**

   - Modals always on top, perf overlay optionally above or below (your choice).
   - No editor clicks while modal is up unless inside modal area.

---

### Phase 6 – Goto Line & Find/Replace modals

**Goal:** Reuse the modal infrastructure for two other high‑value overlays.
**Effort:** **M** (1–3h each; call this **L** in total)
**User impact:** High – complete your basic modal feature set.

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From Helix: small, position‑aware overlays (Goto line dialog, search bar).
- From EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE: use scroll/cursor helpers when jumping to a line.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Goto Line modal.**

   - Add `GotoLineState` & `GotoLineMsg` to `model/ui.rs`:

     ```rust
     pub struct GotoLineState {
         pub input: String,
     }

     pub enum GotoLineMsg {
         InsertChar(char),
         Backspace,
         Confirm,
         Cancel,
     }
     ```

   - Add `CommandId::GotoLine` entry and map it in `execute_command`.
   - Implement `update_goto_line` in `update/ui.rs`:
     - Parse `input` to a line number.
     - Send an appropriate `EditorMsg` or `DocumentMsg` to move the cursor and adjust scroll (using helpers from `EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md`).
   - Render a simple centered smaller modal with one input line and instructions.

2. **Find/Replace modal.**

   - Add `FindReplaceState` with `query`, `replacement`, `mode` (find / replace / replace all), `case_sensitive` flag, etc.
   - Add `FindReplaceMsg` variants for editing the fields and performing operations.
   - Wire into existing search logic in `update/editor.rs` (if you have occurrence selection etc.).
   - Render as a bar either:
     - at the top of the editor (like VS Code), or
     - as a modal dialog like the command palette.

3. **Extend `input.rs` modal routing.**

   - For active `GotoLine` or `FindReplace`, route keys to the corresponding `ModalMsg` instead of `CommandPaletteMsg`.
   - This can be a simple match over `model.ui.active_modal`’s variant.

4. **Add keyboard shortcuts.**

   - `Cmd+L` → `GotoLine`.
   - `Cmd+F` / `Cmd+Shift+F` → `Find` / `Replace`.

---

### Phase 7 – Introduce damage tracking (after UI has stabilized)

**Goal:** Add a modest damage system for partial redraws, focused mostly on the text area and overlays.
**Effort:** **L–XL** (1–3d depending on how far you go)
**User impact:** Better performance on large files / high‑DPI, especially with future features.

**Key patterns to adopt:**

- From Alacritty: **line‑level damage** and frame damage accumulation.
- From Helix: **double‑buffered surfaces** with diff optimization.
- **Skip** for now:
  - Wezterm’s triple‑buffered vertex buffers and GPU‑level optimizations.
  - Wayland `swap_buffers_with_damage` specifics until you actually need platform optimizations.

**Concrete steps & files:**

1. **Define damage types in `view.rs`.**

   - A conservative start:

     ```rust
     #[derive(Default, Clone)]
     pub struct FrameDamage {
         pub full: bool,
         pub rects: Vec<Rect>,
     }

     impl FrameDamage {
         pub fn full() -> Self { Self { full: true, rects: Vec::new() } }
         pub fn add_rect(&mut self, rect: Rect) {
             if !self.full { self.rects.push(rect); }
         }
     }
     ```

   - Optionally, add a line‑range type for text area (like Alacritty’s `LineDamageBounds`).

2. **Store damage in the renderer or model.**

   - Easiest: keep it in `Renderer` (GUI layer):

     ```rust
     pub struct Renderer {
         // existing fields
         frame_damage: FrameDamage,
     }
     ```

   - `App` requests redraws by telling renderer what changed, not by mutating `FrameDamage` directly.

3. **Thread damage information from update functions.**

   - Extend `Cmd` if needed, or add a new enum:

     ```rust
     pub enum Cmd {
         Redraw,
         RedrawRect(Rect),
         RedrawLines { group_id: GroupId, line_range: Range<usize> },
         // ...
     }
     ```

   - Update handlers that do small changes (e.g., cursor move, blinking, scroll) to return more specific `Cmd`s.

4. **Use damage in `Renderer::render_impl`.**

   - Initial step (safe, simple): treat every `Cmd` as `full = true` but keep the type in place.
   - Once stable, optimize:
     - For line‑based damage, only re‑render affected lines in the text area.
     - For overlays, mark just the overlay rect.
   - Only if you want to go further:
     - Wire through platform‑specific damage APIs (e.g., Wayland, macOS).

5. **Guardrails:** behind a feature flag.

   - Add a `cargo` feature or runtime config `--no-damage-tracking`.
   - During initial rollout, default to off, then flip once confident.

---

3) **Rationale and trade-offs**

- **Order of work:** Frame/Widget → Modals → Command Palette → Other Modals → Compositor → Damage.
  - You get internal cleanliness first, then user‑visible features, then performance optimizations.
- **Keep Elm core intact:** No traits, no reactive tree or entity system; all state stays in `model/`, mutations in `update/`.
- **Selective borrowing from other projects:**
  - Zed: borrow focus + modal concepts, not GPUI itself.
  - Helix: borrow compositor & overlay patterns, not the full component trait system.
  - Alacritty: borrow the *shape* of damage tracking, not all platform/game‑engine complexity.
  - Wezterm: skip – their GPU/quad machinery doesn’t map cleanly to your CPU softbuffer path.

---

4) **Risks and guardrails**

- **Risk: modal routing complexity.**
  - Guardrail: Only one `active_modal` at a time, plain enum; routing is one `match` on that enum in `input.rs`.
- **Risk: geometry duplication between input and view.**
  - Guardrail: All layout rects live in one place (e.g., `model/editor_area.rs` or `view/geometry.rs`), shared by both.
- **Risk: damage tracking bugs (tearing, stale pixels).**
  - Guardrail: Introduce damage types early but treat everything as `full` until thoroughly tested; enable partial redraw under a feature flag.
- **Risk: view.rs bloat.**
  - Guardrail: As soon as it exceeds ~1200–1400 LOC again, split into `view/` submodules by widget concern (editor, chrome, modals).

---

5) **When to consider the advanced path**

Revisit a more complex design (e.g., Zed‑style 3‑phase render, full component trait system, plugin architecture) only if:

- You want **live, reactive UI widgets** beyond the editor (e.g., embedded terminals, complex file trees) and `view.rs` starts mirroring a GUI framework.
- You add a **second frontend** (TUI or web), making a trait‑based or multi‑crate design (`core` vs `gui`) worthwhile.
- Profiling shows that with partial redraw and the current painter you’re still **GPU/CPU bound on large files** or ultra‑high‑DPI resolutions.

---

6) **Optional advanced path (outline only)**

If you hit those thresholds:

- Add a **proper 3‑phase pipeline**:
  - `compute_layout(model) -> LayoutTree`
  - `prepaint(&LayoutTree, &mut Cache)` for animations/rasterization
  - `paint(&LayoutTree, &mut Frame)`
- Introduce a **`Component` trait** with `fn event(&mut self, ...) -> EventResult` and a small tree of components for tab bar, editor panes, sidebars.
- Move to a **GPU backend** (wgpu + glyphon or similar) but reuse the same `Frame`/Painter API at the view layer.

Until then, the phased plan above will keep the system simple, testable, and incremental, while giving you the core UX improvements you want (modals, command palette, overlays, and later, faster renders).
```

## Assistant

### Current Structure Explained

```
src/
├── model/              # State (the "M" in Elm's Model-Update-View)
│   ├── document.rs     # Document, EditOperation, Rope buffer
│   ├── editor.rs       # EditorState, Cursor, Selection, Viewport
│   ├── editor_area.rs  # EditorArea, groups, tabs, layout tree
│   ├── status_bar.rs   # StatusBar state
│   └── ui.rs           # UiState (blink, transient messages)
│
├── update/             # State mutation handlers (the "U" in Elm)
│   ├── app.rs          # File I/O, window resize
│   ├── document.rs     # Text editing, clipboard
│   ├── editor.rs       # Cursor movement, selection
│   ├── layout.rs       # Splits, tabs, groups
│   └── ui.rs           # Status bar, cursor blink
│
├── view.rs             # Rendering (the "V" in Elm)
├── input.rs            # Keyboard/mouse → Msg mapping
├── app.rs              # winit ApplicationHandler
├── messages.rs         # Msg, EditorMsg, DocumentMsg, etc.
├── commands.rs         # Cmd enum (Redraw, SaveFile, etc.)
├── main.rs             # Entry point + ~669 lines of inline tests
└── ...
```

The **model/** folder exists but is a subfolder, so it's easy to miss.

---

### Naming Critique

| Current | Issue |
|---------|-------|
| `update/` | "Update" sounds like a verb/action, not a module. Elm calls it "update" but in Rust codebases `handlers/`, `reducers/`, or `logic/` are more common |
| `messages.rs` | Fine, but `msg.rs` or `events.rs` could work too |
| `commands.rs` | Contains `Cmd` enum (effects), not "commands" in the command palette sense—potential confusion |

---

### Suggested Alternatives

#### Option A: Redux-style naming
```
src/
├── state/          # Was: model/
├── reducers/       # Was: update/
├── actions.rs      # Was: messages.rs (or events.rs)
├── effects.rs      # Was: commands.rs
└── view.rs
```

#### Option B: Domain-centric (my recommendation)
```
src/
├── core/               # Library: pure logic, no I/O
│   ├── state/          # Was: model/
│   ├── handlers/       # Was: update/
│   ├── msg.rs          # Was: messages.rs
│   └── effects.rs      # Was: commands.rs
│
├── gui/                # Binary: winit/softbuffer frontend
│   ├── app.rs
│   ├── input.rs
│   ├── view.rs
│   ├── overlay.rs
│   └── perf.rs
│
├── lib.rs              # Exports core/
└── main.rs             # Entry point only
```

#### Option C: Flat but explicit
```
src/
├── state/              # Was: model/
├── logic/              # Was: update/ (or "handlers/", "mutations/")
├── render/             # Was: view.rs → now a module
├── events.rs           # Was: messages.rs
├── effects.rs          # Was: commands.rs
└── ...
```

---

### Moving Tests Out

Currently you have:
- `tests/` – integration tests (already external) ✓
- `src/main.rs` – ~669 lines of inline `#[test]` ✗
- `src/model/editor_area.rs`, `src/overlay.rs`, `src/theme.rs` – inline tests

**Recommended approach:**

1. **Keep unit tests in `tests/`** (you already have this structure)
2. **Move inline tests from `src/*.rs` to `tests/`**

```
tests/
├── common/mod.rs           # Test helpers
├── cursor_movement.rs      # ✓ exists
├── editor_area.rs          # Move from src/model/editor_area.rs
├── main_integration.rs     # Move from src/main.rs
├── overlay.rs              # Move from src/overlay.rs
├── theme.rs                # Move from src/theme.rs
└── ...
```

This requires making tested items `pub` in `lib.rs`, which you mostly have.

---

### My Recommendation

**Option B (domain-centric)** with explicit `core/` vs `gui/` separation:

```
src/
├── core/
│   ├── state/          # AppModel, Document, EditorState, etc.
│   ├── handlers/       # Pure state transitions (was update/)
│   ├── msg.rs          # Message enums
│   └── effects.rs      # Cmd enum
│
├── gui/
│   ├── app.rs          # ApplicationHandler
│   ├── input.rs        # Key/mouse → Msg
│   ├── view.rs         # Rendering
│   └── perf.rs         # Debug overlay
│
├── lib.rs
└── main.rs

tests/                  # All tests here
├── common/
├── handlers/           # Test state transitions
├── rendering/          # Snapshot/visual tests
└── integration/        # End-to-end
```

This makes the architecture immediately obvious and prepares you for a potential TUI frontend later.

Want me to draft a migration plan for this restructure?

## Assistant

### Alternative 1: Elm-Faithful with Clarity

Keep the Elm terminology but make it more visible at the top level:

```
src/
├── model/              # State types (already exists)
├── update/             # Rename to "msg_handlers/" or keep as-is
├── view/               # Promote view.rs to module
├── msg.rs              # Renamed from messages.rs (shorter)
├── cmd.rs              # Renamed from commands.rs (matches Elm's Cmd)
├── runtime/            # NEW: winit glue
│   ├── app.rs
│   ├── input.rs
│   └── perf.rs
└── main.rs
```

**Pitch:** Stays true to Elm. Anyone who knows Elm immediately gets it. The `runtime/` folder clearly separates "framework stuff" from "editor logic."

---

### Alternative 2: Feature-Sliced (Vertical Modules)

Group by feature/domain instead of by architectural layer:

```
src/
├── editor/             # Core editing
│   ├── state.rs        # EditorState, Cursor, Selection
│   ├── handlers.rs     # Cursor movement, selection logic
│   └── render.rs       # Text area rendering
│
├── document/           # Text buffer
│   ├── state.rs        # Document, EditOperation
│   ├── handlers.rs     # Insert, delete, undo/redo
│   └── render.rs       # (minimal, just provides text)
│
├── layout/             # Splits, tabs, groups
│   ├── state.rs
│   ├── handlers.rs
│   └── render.rs
│
├── chrome/             # Tab bar, status bar, gutter
│   ├── state.rs
│   ├── handlers.rs
│   └── render.rs
│
├── app.rs              # winit glue, entry orchestration
├── msg.rs              # All Msg enums
├── cmd.rs              # Cmd enum
└── main.rs
```

**Pitch:** When you're working on "tabs," everything is in `layout/`. No jumping between `model/editor_area.rs`, `update/layout.rs`, and `view.rs`. Scales well as features grow.

---

### Alternative 3: Simple & Flat (No Nesting)

Minimize folder depth, use prefixes for grouping:

```
src/
├── state_document.rs
├── state_editor.rs
├── state_layout.rs
├── state_ui.rs
├── state.rs            # Re-exports + AppModel
│
├── handle_document.rs
├── handle_editor.rs
├── handle_layout.rs
├── handle_ui.rs
├── handle.rs           # Dispatcher
│
├── render.rs           # All rendering (or render_*.rs if it grows)
├── input.rs
├── app.rs
├── msg.rs
├── cmd.rs
└── main.rs
```

**Pitch:** Zero nesting, everything in `src/`. IDE autocomplete works great with prefixes (`state_`, `handle_`, `render_`). Easy to navigate. No `mod.rs` ceremony.

---

### Comparison Table

| Aspect | Alt 1 (Elm-Faithful) | Alt 2 (Feature-Sliced) | Alt 3 (Flat) |
|--------|---------------------|------------------------|--------------|
| **Familiarity** | Elm devs love it | React/Redux devs love it | Go/C devs love it |
| **File count** | Medium | Higher | Higher but flat |
| **Navigation** | By layer | By feature | By prefix |
| **Scales to...** | ~20 files well | Large features | ~30 files before chaos |
| **Refactor ease** | Move between layers | Self-contained slices | Rename files |

---

Which direction appeals to you? Or should I mix elements from multiple?

│
├── runtime/            # NEW: winit/platform glue
│   ├── mod.rs

│   └── perf.rs         # Move from src/perf.rs
│

tests/                  # All tests here
├── common/
├── model/              # Tests for model types
│   └── editor_area.rs

```

### Migration Steps

| Step | Action | Effort |
|------|--------|--------|
| 1 | Rename `messages.rs` → `msg.rs`, `commands.rs` → `cmd.rs` | 5 min |
| 2 | Create `runtime/` and move `app.rs`, `input.rs`, `perf.rs` | 15 min |
| 3 | Create `view/` module from `view.rs` + `overlay.rs` | 30 min |
| 4 | Move inline tests from `src/main.rs` → `tests/integration/` | 20 min |
| 5 | Move inline tests from `src/model/editor_area.rs` → `tests/model/` | 10 min |
| 6 | Move inline tests from `src/overlay.rs`, `src/theme.rs` → `tests/view/` | 10 min |
| 7 | Update `lib.rs` exports and fix imports | 15 min |

**Total:** ~2 hours

  "content": "# GUI Cleanup & Architecture Improvement Plan\n\n**Status:** 🚧 In Progress  \n**Created:** 2025-12-07\n\nThis document consolidates the GUI improvement roadmap, including Elm-style directory restructuring and the phased rendering/modal system improvements.\n\n---\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n1. [Research Summary](#research-summary)\n\n---\n\n## Phase 0 – Elm-Style Directory Restructure\n\n**Goal:** Reorganize codebase to clearly reflect Elm's Model-Update-View architecture; move all tests to `tests/`.  \n**Effort:** M (1.5–2h)  \n**User Impact:** None (internal restructure)\n\n### Current Structure\n\n```\nsrc/\n├── model/              # ✓ State types (exists)\n├── update/             # ✓ State handlers (exists)\n├── view.rs             # Rendering (flat file)\n├── overlay.rs          # Overlay utilities (flat file)\n├── app.rs              # winit glue\n├── input.rs            # Key/mouse handling\n├── perf.rs             # Debug overlay\n├── messages.rs         # Msg enums\n├── commands.rs         # Cmd enum\n├── main.rs             # Entry + ~669 lines of tests\n└── ...\n```\n\n### Target Structure\n\n```\nsrc/\n├── model/              # ✓ Keep as-is\n│   ├── document.rs\n│   ├── editor.rs\n│   ├── editor_area.rs\n│   ├── status_bar.rs\n│   ├── ui.rs\n│   └── mod.rs\n│\n├── update/             # ✓ Keep as-is\n│   ├── app.rs\n│   ├── document.rs\n│   ├── editor.rs\n│   ├── layout.rs\n│   ├── ui.rs\n│   └── mod.rs\n│\n├── view/               # NEW: Promote to module\n│   ├── mod.rs          # Renderer struct, render_root()\n│   ├── editor.rs       # render_editor_area, render_text_area, render_gutter\n│   ├── chrome.rs       # render_tab_bar, render_status_bar\n│   └── overlay.rs      # Move from src/overlay.rs\n│\n├── runtime/            # NEW: winit/platform glue\n│   ├── mod.rs\n│   ├── app.rs          # Move from src/app.rs\n│   ├── input.rs        # Move from src/input.rs\n│   └── perf.rs         # Move from src/perf.rs\n│\n├── msg.rs              # Rename from messages.rs\n├── cmd.rs              # Rename from commands.rs\n├── theme.rs            # Keep\n├── util.rs             # Keep\n├── lib.rs              # Exports model, update, view, msg, cmd\n└── main.rs             # Entry point only (~20 lines)\n\ntests/                  # All tests here\n├── common/             # Test helpers (exists)\n├── model/              # Tests for model types\n│   └── editor_area.rs  # Move from src/model/editor_area.rs\n├── view/               # Tests for rendering\n│   ├── overlay.rs      # Move from src/overlay.rs\n│   └── theme.rs        # Move from src/theme.rs\n├── integration/        # End-to-end tests\n│   └── main.rs         # Move from src/main.rs\n└── ... (existing test files)\n```\n\n### Migration Steps\n\n| Step | Action | Files |\n|------|--------|-------|\n| 1 | Rename `messages.rs` → `msg.rs` | `src/messages.rs` |\n| 2 | Rename `commands.rs` → `cmd.rs` | `src/commands.rs` |\n| 3 | Create `runtime/` module | `src/runtime/mod.rs` |\n| 4 | Move `app.rs` → `runtime/app.rs` | `src/app.rs` |\n| 5 | Move `input.rs` → `runtime/input.rs` | `src/input.rs` |\n| 6 | Move `perf.rs` → `runtime/perf.rs` | `src/perf.rs` |\n| 7 | Create `view/` module | `src/view/mod.rs` |\n| 8 | Move `view.rs` content → `view/mod.rs` | `src/view.rs` |\n| 9 | Move `overlay.rs` → `view/overlay.rs` | `src/overlay.rs` |\n| 10 | Move inline tests from `src/main.rs` → `tests/integration/main.rs` | `src/main.rs` |\n| 11 | Move inline tests from `src/model/editor_area.rs` → `tests/model/editor_area.rs` | `src/model/editor_area.rs` |\n| 12 | Move inline tests from `src/overlay.rs` → `tests/view/overlay.rs` | `src/overlay.rs` |\n| 13 | Move inline tests from `src/theme.rs` → `tests/view/theme.rs` | `src/theme.rs` |\n| 14 | Update `lib.rs` exports | `src/lib.rs` |\n| 15 | Fix all imports throughout codebase | Various |\n\n### Why This Structure\n\n- **Elm-faithful**: `model/`, `update/`, `view/` are immediately recognizable\n- **Clear separation**: `runtime/` isolates platform-specific winit glue from pure logic\n- **Shorter names**: `msg.rs` and `cmd.rs` match Elm terminology\n- **Tests external**: All `#[test]` code lives in `tests/`, making `src/` cleaner\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1 – Frame/Painter Abstraction\n\n**Goal:** Centralize drawing primitives; stop indexing pixel buffer directly everywhere.  \n**Effort:** M (1–3h)  \n**User Impact:** None (internal refactor, unblocks everything else)\n\n**Files to modify:**\n\n- `src/view/mod.rs` – Add `Frame` and `TextPainter` structs\n- `src/view/overlay.rs` – Migrate to use `Frame` helpers\n\n**Steps:**\n\n1. Add `Frame` struct with `clear()`, `fill_rect()`, `blend_pixel()` methods\n2. Add `TextPainter` wrapper for fontdue + glyph cache\n3. Wrap `Renderer::render_impl()` to create `Frame` from softbuffer\n4. Migrate existing pixel loops to `Frame` methods (status bar → tab bar → gutter → text area)\n5. Migrate overlay to take `&mut Frame` instead of raw buffer\n\n```rust\npub struct Frame<'a> {\n    pub buffer: &'a mut [u32],\n    pub width: usize,\n    pub height: usize,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Frame<'a> {\n    pub fn clear(&mut self, color: u32);\n    pub fn fill_rect(&mut self, rect: Rect, color: u32);\n    pub fn blend_pixel(&mut self, x: usize, y: usize, color: u32);\n}\n\npub struct TextPainter<'a> {\n    font: &'a Font,\n    glyph_cache: &'a mut GlyphCache,\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2 – Widget Extraction & Geometry Centralization\n\n**Goal:** Transform monolithic render function into composable widget functions.  \n**Effort:** M–L (3–8h, incremental)  \n**User Impact:** Invisible, improves maintainability\n\n**Files to modify:**\n\n- `src/view/mod.rs` – Extract widget functions\n- `src/view/editor.rs` – Editor area widgets\n- `src/view/chrome.rs` – Tab bar, status bar\n- `src/model/editor_area.rs` or new `src/view/geometry.rs` – Centralize geometry helpers\n\n**Steps:**\n\n1. Extract high-level widget renderers:\n   - `render_root()` – orchestrates all rendering\n   - `render_editor_area()` – groups + splitters\n   - `render_editor_group()` – tab bar + editor pane\n   - `render_tab_bar()`, `render_gutter()`, `render_text_area()`\n   - `render_splitters()`, `render_status_bar()`\n\n2. Centralize geometry helpers (from EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md):\n   - `compute_visible_lines()`\n   - Line/column ↔ pixel conversions\n   - Gutter width computation\n\n3. Unify hit-testing geometry between `runtime/input.rs` and `view/`\n   - Single source of truth for tab bar rect, text area rect, gutter rect per group\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3 – Basic Modal/Focus System\n\n**Goal:** Add minimal modal overlay + focus capture mechanism.  \n**Effort:** M (1–3h)  \n**User Impact:** Foundation only (add placeholder modal to test)\n\n**Files to modify:**\n\n- `src/model/ui.rs` – Add `ModalState` enum, extend `UiState`\n- `src/msg.rs` – Add `ModalMsg`, extend `UiMsg`\n- `src/update/ui.rs` – Handle modal state changes\n- `src/runtime/input.rs` – Implement keyboard focus capture\n- `src/view/mod.rs` – Add `render_modals()` with dim background\n\n**Model changes:**\n\n```rust\npub enum ModalState {\n    CommandPalette(CommandPaletteState),\n    GotoLine(GotoLineState),\n    FindReplace(FindReplaceState),\n}\n\npub struct UiState {\n    pub active_modal: Option<ModalState>,\n    // existing fields...\n}\n```\n\n**Input routing (focus capture):**\n\n```rust\npub fn handle_key(model: &AppModel, event: &KeyEvent) -> Option<Msg> {\n    if model.ui.active_modal.is_some() {\n        return handle_modal_key(model, event);\n    }\n    handle_editor_key(model, event)\n}\n```\n\n**Rendering (layer 2):**\n\n```rust\nfn render_modals(frame: &mut Frame, text: &mut TextPainter, ui: &UiState, theme: &Theme) {\n    let Some(modal) = &ui.active_modal else { return };\n\n    // 1. Dim background (Zed-style BlockMouse)\n    let dim_color = 0x80000000;\n    for pixel in frame.buffer.iter_mut() {\n        *pixel = blend_pixel(dim_color, *pixel);\n    }\n\n    // 2. Render modal content\n    match modal { /* ... */ }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4 – Command Palette (Full Vertical Slice)\n\n**Goal:** Ship real, useful command palette on modal system.  \n**Effort:** L (1–2d)  \n**User Impact:** HIGH – visible feature, anchors modal system\n\n**Files to create/modify:**\n\n- `src/cmd.rs` – Add `CommandId` enum, `COMMANDS` registry\n- `src/msg.rs` – Add `update_command_palette()`\n- `src/runtime/input.rs` – Extend modal key routing, add Cmd+P binding\n- `src/view/mod.rs` – Add `render_command_palette()`\n\n**Command registry:**\n\n```rust\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum CommandId {\n    ShowCommandPalette,\n    GotoLine,\n    SaveFile,\n    SplitRight,\n    SplitDown,\n    Undo,\n static COMMANDS: &[Command] = &[/* ... */];\n```\n\n**Execute command flow:**\n\n```rust\nfn execute_command(model: &mut AppModel, cmd_id: CommandId) -> Option<Cmd> {\n    match cmd_id {\n Command Palette           | L (1–2d)    | Phase 3      | **P1** (high user value)   |\n| 5. Compositor/Mouse          | M (1–3h)    | Phase 3      | **P2** (polish)            |\n| 6. Goto/Find Modals          | L (1d)      | Phase 4      | **P1** (high user value)   |\n| 7. Damage Tracking           | L–XL (1–3d) | Phase 2      | **P3** (optimization)      |\n\n**Total estimated effort:** 2–3 weeks of focused work\n\n---\n\n## Research Summary\n\nBased on analysis of Zed (GPUI), Helix, Alacritty, and Wezterm:\n\n| Project        | Key Patterns Adopted                                                          | Patterns Skipped                                            |\n| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |\n| **Zed (GPUI)** | Modal focus capture, FocusHandle concept, hitbox blocking for overlays        | Full 3-phase render, entity system, DispatchTree complexity |\n| **Helix**      | Compositor with layer stack, overlay wrapper, EventResult::Consumed/Ignored   | Full trait-based Component system, callback pattern         |\n| **Alacritty**  | Line-level damage tracking, frame damage accumulation, double-buffered damage | Platform-specific Wayland optimizations                     |\n| **Wezterm**    | Layer-based z-ordering concept                                                | Quad-based GPU rendering, triple-buffered vertices          |\n\n---\n\n## When to Consider Advanced Path\n\nRevisit more complex designs (Zed-style 3-phase render, trait-based components, plugin architecture) only if:\n\n1. You want **reactive UI widgets** beyond the editor (embedded terminals, complex file trees)\n2. You add a **second frontend** (TUI or web), making trait-based design worthwhile\n3. Profiling shows **still CPU/GPU bound** after damage tracking on large files\n\nUntil then, this phased plan keeps the system simple, testable, and incremental.\n\n---\n\n## References\n\n- [GUI-REVIEW-FINDINGS.md](archived/GUI-REVIEW-FINDINGS.md) – Original comprehensive analysis (archived)\n- [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) – Text editor UI geometry reference\n- [ORGANIZATION-CODEBASE.md](archived/ORGANIZATION-CODEBASE.md) – Previous restructuring work\n"
}
```
